The error indicates that the system is trying to write to a read-only file system:\nError: [Errno 30] Read-only file system: 'logfile\_user\_audit.log'\nThis suggests that the Cybereason API server is having trouble accessing or writing to the audit log file because the file system is mounted as read-only.\nPossible solutions:\nCheck file system permissions: The directory where the audit logs are stored might need write permissions\nVerify server configuration: The Cybereason API server might need to be configured to use a different log location\nCheck disk space: The file system might be full and mounted as read-only as a protective measure\nContact system administrator: This appears to be an infrastructure issue that may require administrative access to resolve | Yes | question- i think the problem is that it try to download with this tool and it dont know to where, can i write code that make claude produce a link to download?  answer- You're absolutely right! The API is designed to generate files on the server side, but without proper download handling configured in your environment.\nHere are a few potential solutions:\n\nCheck if there's a web interface: Many Cybereason deployments have a web console where you can access audit logs directly through the UI.\nUse a different approach: If you have direct access to the Cybereason server, you might be able to access the audit logs through:\n\nSSH/command line access to the server\nDirect database queries (if you have database access)\nLog files on the file system\n\n\nContact your Cybereason administrator: They may need to configure proper download paths or provide alternative access methods for audit logs. | NaN | Chen | NaN | NaN | NaN | NaN | NaN | NaN | NaN | NaN |

Here’s a comprehensive list of sensor actions you can perform in Cybereason:\n\nCore sensor lifecycle and maintenance\n- Restart sensor: Reboots the Cybereason sensor service on selected machines.\n- Upgrade sensor: Triggers an upgrade to the target sensor version.\n- Start/Stop scan: Initiates or stops an on-demand sensor scan (argument required, e.g., quick/full as applicable).\n- Start/Stop collection: Enables or disables enhanced forensic collection on sensors.\n- Abort operation: Cancels an in-progress batch operation by its batch ID.\n\nProtection modes and controls\n- Set anti-ransomware mode: Configure ransomware protection (e.g., enable/disable/protect/monitor).\n- Set anti-malware mode: Configure anti-malware settings, including signatures mode and AI detect/prevent modes.\n- Set Application Control mode: Enable/disable/monitor app control per policy.\n- Set PowerShell protection mode: Configure PowerShell protection (block/monitor/disable).\n\nPolicy, grouping, and tagging\n- Assign policy to sensors: Apply a specific sensor policy to a list of sensors (optionally keep manual overrides).\n- Add sensor to group / Remove sensor from group: Manage group membership for sensors.\n- Add/Update/Remove sensor tags: Manage tags on machines/sensors to aid filtering and policy assignment.\n\nLogs and forensics\n- Retrieve sensor logs: Pull logs from specified sensors.\n- Download sensor logs (by log ID): Download previously retrieved logs (ZIP).\n- File search on sensors: Search for files across sensors with filter criteria.\n\nSensor inventory management\n- Archive / Unarchive sensors: Archive inactive/retired devices or bring them back.\n- Remove sensor from list / Restore removed sensor: Soft-remove or restore a sensor from the console.\n- Delete sensor from list: Permanently delete sensor entry from the list (use with care).\n\nNetwork isolation and containment (context-dependent)\n- Isolate machine (malop context): Isolate an endpoint involved in a MalOp.\n- Isolation rules (network-level): Create/Update/Delete rules to block specific IP/port/direction.\n\nIf you meant “recent sensor actions that were executed” rather than the available actions, I can pull that from audit logs—just let me know the time range.\n\nWould you like me to execute any of these?
